The D105_3sigma_note entries I can see from registry are:

1. Line 1880: "True 3sigma recompute. threshold_onset dropped 8/8->2/8. K4 dropped 6/8->1/8. PermG/TY unchanged." — This must be for one of the alpha sweep experiments (need to identify which)
2. Line 1915: "True 3sigma recompute. threshold_onset dropped 8/8->2/8. K4 dropped 6/8->2/8. PermG/TY unchanged."


3. Line 2011 marks the C4 experiments with a D105 backfill using true 3sigma and fdr_permethod across an 8-pair unified setup with token_entropy and ece as canary metrics paired against d1, d2, d3, and mauve downstream metrics.
4. Lines 2052, 2097, and 2173 repeat the same generic D105 backfill pattern.
5. Line 2127 shows another true 3sigma recompute where threshold_onset dropped 6/8->0/8 and K4 dropped 6/8->2/8 with PermG/TY unchanged.
6. Line 2899 appears to be cut off.

Looking at the subsequent recompute results, I'm seeing a pattern across multiple lines where threshold_onset consistently drops to 0/8 in most cases, K4 shows varying drops (ranging from 2/8 to 6/8), and PermG/TY remains stable throughout all these true 3sigma recomputes.

But I'm realizing these entries are from Table 7 experiments, not the alpha sweep dose-response experiments I'm actually trying to track. I need to search the registry more carefully for the alpha sweep specific entries and their corresponding D105 notes.

There's something puzzling here though — the D105 notes consistently state "PermG/TY unchanged," which suggests D105 only affects threshold_onset, yet the Director's reference shows TY=0/8 for certain alpha ranges while the D111 audit shows TY=3-4/8. I'm wondering if the D111 audit values already incorporate the D105 effects, or if these are separate measurements I'm conflating.

The alpha sweep experiments themselves were processed through the batch_analysis script, and the timing matters — some of these experiments ran before D105 was applied as a correction, while others ran after. The real question is whether the server-side analysis files that generated the D111 audit values reflect the original pre-D105 state or a post-D105 corrected state, and that depends entirely on when each experiment was actually analyzed.

The Director is saying both threshold and TY should be 0/8, but the D111 audit shows threshold=5/8 and TY=3/8 from the server files. If D105 only touched the threshold calculation, why would TY also be wrong? Either D105 affected TY as well for the alpha sweep experiments, or the alpha sweep analysis used an older buggy TY implementation that was never corrected, or the Director's reference values come from a completely separate reanalysis that fixed both metrics.

Looking back at the roadmap, I need to clarify what actually happened with the alpha sweep dose-response curve versus the Table 7 cross-model experiments — the D105 3σ correction was explicitly for Table 7, but the alpha sweep may have followed a different correction path entirely. I'm noticing a contradiction: D105 only affects threshold_onset, not TY, but the Director's reference values show TY=0/8 for lower alpha values while the D111 audit reports TY=3/8 or 4/8. Since neither D111's direction check nor D105's 3σ correction should change TY values, I need to figure out what's causing this discrepancy between the two sources. I'm wondering if the D105 reanalysis script recalculated TY with different parameters—maybe a different lag order or significance threshold—rather than just updating the backfill data. The registry for the alpha sweep experiments might have been updated with these D105-corrected values, which would explain the discrepancy with what the D111 audit shows. Let me check what the registry actually contains for those alpha sweep entries.

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I can see the pattern. The registry entries for alpha sweep experiments contain:

**α=0.05 (alpha_sweep_a005_s42_v3)**:
- threshold_onset_fwd: 5/8
- ty_granger_fwd: 3/8
- Note: D111 direction-checked: naive 7/8→3/8, diff 3/8→0/8, K≥3 3/8→0/8, K≥4 0/8→0/8


**α=0.10 (alpha_sweep_a010_s42_fp32)** shows similar onset and granger results, though the direction-checked metrics shift slightly with naive improving to 4/8 and the K≥3 constraint yielding 2/8 before dropping to zero at K≥4. **α=0.25 (alpha_sweep_a025_s42_fp32)** appears to follow the same pattern with threshold_onset_fwd at 5/8, though the entry cuts off mid-note.

The registry values align with what the D111 audit found—both showing 5/8 threshold and 3/8 TY across these alpha settings—but the Director's expected values are strikingly different, claiming 0/8 for α=0.05, 2/8 for α=0.10, and 2/8 for α=0.25. I'm trying to understand where this discrepancy originates.

The Director might be referencing a separate D105 correction specific to the alpha sweep experiments that never made it back into the registry. Or perhaps the Director is recalculating the D105 3σ threshold values from scratch. The key insight is that the registry's 5/8 value was computed using the pre-D105 2σ significance standard, whereas D105 shifted to 3σ, which would naturally reduce how many pairs cross the significance threshold. So the registry for these alpha sweep experiments was apparently never updated to reflect the D105 change.

Now I'm puzzled by the TY discrepancy. The Director reports TY=0/8 across α=0.05-0.35, but the registry shows TY=3-4/8. The D105 notes explicitly state that PermG and TY remain unchanged, so D105 shouldn't explain this gap. I need to look more carefully at whether the D105 notes apply uniformly across all experiments or if there's a distinction between how Table 7 and alpha sweep experiments were handled.

Actually, I think I've been misinterpreting the D105 notes. When they say "PermG/TY unchanged" for Table 7 experiments, that's describing the outcome at α=1.00 where the signal is so strong that even with 3σ thresholds, TY stays significant. But D105 itself is described as "global true 3σ" — meaning it applies the 3σ threshold universally across all significance tests, including TY Granger. So D105 would affect TY calculations in the alpha sweep experiments, just not visibly in Table 7 where the effect is already robust. This could explain why the Director's TY values differ from what's in the registry. For the missing entries in the CSV, I need to apply D105 3σ corrections to the D111 audit values, but the per-pair tables only show T/F results without raw z-scores, so I can't determine which specific pairs would change significance when moving from 2σ to 3σ thresholds. The Director also provided K≥3 counts for these alpha values, which suggests they computed this metric from the D111 audit data using the earlier threshold and TY values. Looking at the D111 K values across the five methods, I can work backwards to understand what the Director was tracking.

For α=0.50, the D111 audit shows K≥3=6/8 when I count the pairs, but the Director reports 5/8—there's a discrepancy here that might indicate the D105 threshold changes affected which pairs qualified. For α=0.60 and α=0.75, both the audit and Director agree on K≥3=6/8, which is consistent. The key question is whether D105 reduced the threshold in a way that would drop some pairs from significant to non-significant at α=0.50, explaining why the Director's count is lower than what the raw D111 data suggests. I'm realizing the Director might have referenced a specific worker that performed the D105 reanalysis across all experiments, including the alpha sweep—let me check what that worker produced to find the correct 3σ threshold and TY values.

The key note: "PermG/TY 完全不受影响（符合预期）" — this says PermG/TY are completely unaffected by D105, as expected. This is for Table 7 experiments at α=1.00.


But the Director's results show TY=0/8 for low-α sweeps, which contradicts the "unchanged" claim unless D105 applies differently to those experiments. I'm realizing that D105's 3σ threshold might only affect threshold_onset specifically, not TY's chi-squared test. Yet that doesn't explain why the Director shows TY=0/8 when the D111 audit found TY=3-4/8 — there's a discrepancy I need to resolve.

Looking at the Director's reference table more carefully, at α=0.05 all methods except naive show 0/8, which actually makes sense since there's minimal contamination signal at that level. The D111 audit showing TY=3/8 at α=0.05 looks like false positives. I suspect there's a D105 correction for TY that I'm missing in the script.

Rather than chase down every pipeline detail, I should take a practical approach: use the Director's provided values as authoritative, fill in the missing entries by finding the actual test results, and add the three missing α values. Looking back at the registry entry for alpha_sweep_a050_s42_fp32, I need to extract the D105 metrics from where I left off reading.

So the α=0.30 experiment finished at 20:33, which means its analysis ran after the D105 fix was deployed. But here's the complication: the analysis is embedded in the training pipeline itself, not a separate step. If the training script was already running when the fix was applied at 16:28, whether it uses the fixed code depends on when the analysis module gets imported—Python loads modules at import time, so if the analysis code was already loaded before the fix, it would use the old version.

Looking at the specifics, the D105 fix modified the `_onset_gen` function in `method_ablation.py`. Since `iterative_train.py` imports that module at startup, the already-running α=0.30 process would have loaded the old code before the fix was applied, meaning its analysis likely still used the pre-fix 2σ threshold.

But comparing the results between α=0.30 and α=0.35 in the registry shows different patterns in their metrics, which suggests something else might be at play here.

Looking at the timestamps more carefully: α=0.35 started at 2026-05-19T14:37 and finished at 2026-05-19T18:59, while the D105 fix was applied around 16:28. Since the training takes roughly 4.5 hours, the analysis would have run near completion time, but the import happened at the start when the old code was still in place.

But here's the puzzle — the field names "K_ge3_3sigma" and "K_ge4_3sigma" explicitly reference 3σ, which suggests the analysis used the fixed code. 你报告 OPT-1.3B s43 K≥3=6/8, D111 direction=8/8 PASS。**Reviewer 已独立验证这是错的**：

- K≥4: **0/8**（不是 4/8）
- PermG: 6/8 ✅（不受影响）

**立即修正 registry** 中 opt13b_s43 的 K≥3, K≥4, d111_direction_ok 值。

  "task_prompt": "## 任务：D111 方向检查审计 — Table 7 实验 Batch 2 (4 个实验)\n\n### 背景\nD111 规则：naive_xcorr 和 diff_xcorr 必须 peak_lag > 0 才算 forward direction。Table 7 的 K≥3/K≥4 值可能是 pre-D111 的（未检查方向），需要逐 pair 审计。\n\n**关键 bug 警告**：之前的分析 Worker 把 `d111_direction_ok: 0` 误读为\"功能未启用\"而非\"0 个 pair 方向正确\"。你必须正确解读：\n- `d111_direction_ok: 0` = 零个 pair 通过方向检查\n- `d111_direction_ok: N` = N 个 pair 通过方向检查\n\n### 实验列表（全部在 westd-17855）\n\n1. **GPT-2m s42**\n   - 路径: `/root/autodl-tmp/distrib_canary_collapse/results/generalize/gen_wiki_gpt2m_a100_s42_v4/` 或 `gen_wiki_gpt2m_a100_s42/`\n   - 当前 registry: PermG=6/8, K≥3=6/8, K≥4=2/8\n\n2. **GPT-2m s43**\n   - 路径: `/root/autodl-tmp/distrib_canary_collapse/results/generalize/gen_gpt2m_a100_s43_fp32/` 或 `gen_wiki_gpt2m_a100_s43/`\n   - 当前 registry: PermG=6/8, K≥3=6/8, K≥4=4/8\n\n3. **OPT-1.3B s42**\n   - 路径: `/root/autodl-tmp/distrib_canary_collapse/results/generalize/gen_opt13b_wiki_a100_s42_fp32/` 或 `opt1b_wiki_a100_s42/`\n   - 当前 registry: PermG=6/8, K≥3=6/8, K≥4=2/8\n\n4. **Padding-410M s42**\n   - 路径: `/root/autodl-tmp/distrib_canary_collapse/results/padding_robustness/a100_s42_fp32/`\n   - 当前 registry: PermG=6/8, K≥3=6/8, K≥4=6/8\n\n### 方法\n\n对每个实验：\n1. SSH 到 westd-17855\n2. 首先尝试读 `analysis/d105_analysis.json`——检查 summary 中的 `d111_direction_ok` 字段值\n3. 如果没有 d105_analysis.json，找 `analysis/5method_leadlag.json` 或 `analysis/unified_5method.json`\n4. 读取 8 个 pair: canary={token_entropy, ece} × downstream={distinct_1, distinct_2, distinct_3, mauve}\n5. 对每个 pair 提取:\n   - naive_xcorr: peak_lag, significant → **naive_fwd = significant AND peak_lag > 0**（peak_lag=0 也算失败！）\n   - diff_in_diff_xcorr: peak_lag, significant_fdr → **diff_fwd = significant_fdr AND peak_lag > 0**\n   - threshold_onset: significant（检查 significant_3sigma 字段）\n   - permutation_granger: significant AND direction=fwd\n   - toda_yamamoto: significant_fdr（或 significant）\n6. 计算 K = sum(naive_fwd, diff_fwd, threshold, permG, TY) per pair\n7. K≥3 = count(pairs where K >= 3) / 8\n8. K≥4 = count(pairs where K >= 4) / 8\n\n### 路径查找\n\n如果标准路径不存在，用 find 命令：\n```bash\nfind /root/autodl-tmp/distrib_canary_collapse/results -maxdepth 3 -name \"5method_leadlag.json\" -o -name \"d105_analysis.json\" -o -name \"unified_5method.json\" | grep -i \"gpt2m\\|opt1\\|padding\"\n```\n\n### 输出\n\n写审计报告到: `/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_4/projects/distrib_canary_collapse/artifacts/table7_d111_audit_batch2.md`\n\n格式：每个实验一个 per-pair 表 + summary，包含 old vs new 对比。\n\n### 注意\n- 只用 ssh_read_only，不修改任何文件\n- peak_lag = 0 不算 forward（必须 > 0）\n- peak_lag < 0 不算 forward\n- d111_direction_ok 字段值为数字 N = N 个 pair 方向正确，0 = 零个正确（不是\"未启用\"）\n",

_source line 2531 · 2026-05-20T01:27:05.152Z_

[Director 指令] **🚨 Table 7 审计方法学错误 — 必须联合 D105+D111 重算 K**

Reviewer 发现 Batch 1 审计的 K 值不可用：Worker 用 regular threshold 替代 3sigma threshold 算 K，导致 threshold 投票被高估。例如 Pythia-1.4B s43 K≥3 从 6/8 "上升" 到 7/8——这是假象（regular threshold=8/8 vs 3sigma threshold=0/8 的差异掩盖了 D111 方向剔除）。

**核心问题**：D105（3σ threshold）和 D111（方向检查）从未联合计算过。两个 patch 独立存在，但 K 值需要**同时应用两者**。

**正确的 K 计算规则**（per pair）：
1. **naive_xcorr**: significant=True **AND** peak_lag > 0 → 计 1 票
2. **diff_xcorr**: significant=True **AND** peak_lag > 0 → 计 1 票
3. **threshold_onset**: `significant_3sigma`=True → 计 1 票（**不是** `significant`）
4. **permG**: FDR p < 0.05 → 计 1 票（D086）
5. **TY**: significant=True → 计 1 票

K = 5 票之和。K≥3 = 至少 3 个方法同时通过。

**执行**：
1. **停止 Batch 2 审计**——用同样错误的方法做下去没有意义
2. **写一个统一脚本**（或 Worker 手动逐 pair 计算），对 ALL Table 7 实验的每个 pair 同时应用上述 5 条规则，输出正确的 K 值
3. 对于 `significant_3sigma` 字段缺失的实验：检查 JSON 中是否有 `threshold_gen` 和 `onset_gen` 相关字段可以推导 3σ 判定；如果完全没有，需要用 D105 的 3σ 阈值标准重新从 metrics 时序计算
4. **PermG 统一用 FDR-corrected**（D086），不是 raw p-value

**输出格式**（每个实验）：
```
exp_id: xxx
per_pair:
  - pair: te→d1, naive_fwd: T/F, diff_fwd: T/F, threshold_3σ: T/F, permG_fdr: T/F, TY: T/F, K: N
  - pair: te→d2, ...
summary: naive_fwd=X/8, diff_fwd=X/8, threshold_3σ=X/8, permG_fdr=X/8, TY=X/8, K≥3=X/8, K≥4=X/8
```

这样 Reviewer 可以逐 pair 验证，我们也能一次性得到 Table 7 的权威 K 值。

**优先级**：这比 CSV rebuild 更紧急——CSV 的 dose-response 数据已经 Reviewer PASS，但 Table 7 跨架构 K 值是论文核心表格，目前处于 "未知状态"。

字段 |\n|---|------|---------|-----------|\n| 1 | naive_xcorr | `significant`=True **AND** `peak_lag` > 0 | naive_xcorr.significant, naive_xcorr.peak_lag |\n| 2 | diff_xcorr | `significant_fdr`=True **AND** `peak_lag` > 0 | diff_in_diff_xcorr.significant_fdr, diff_in_diff_xcorr.peak_lag |\n| 3 | threshold_onset | `significant_3sigma`=True | threshold_onset.significant_3sigma（**不是 significant**） |\n| 4 | permG | FDR-corrected p < 0.05 | permutation_granger.p_value_fdr_bh < 0.05 **AND** direction=\"fwd\" |\n| 5 | TY | `significant`=True | toda_yamamoto.significant |\n\n**⚠️ 关键细节：**\n- peak_lag This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   This is an ML research experiment management session for the `distrib_canary_collapse` project targeting EMNLP 2026 (ARR June cycle, DDL ~June 15). The agent manages experiments, dispatches Workers, and coordinates with a Director. The session has three urgent interleaved tasks:
   
   **Task A (P0): Table 7 D105+D111 Unified K-Value Audit** — The most recent and highest priority task. Director/Reviewer discovered that Table 7 cross-architecture experiments have K≥3/K≥4 values computed without jointly applying D105 (3σ threshold) AND D111 (direction check). Two prior audit attempts (Batch 1/2) also failed because they used regular threshold instead of `significant_3sigma`. A unified Worker has been dispatched to correctly recompute K for all 13 Table 7 experiments using the 5-rule voting system.
   
   **Task B (P0): CSV Rebuild with D105+D111 Corrections** — The dose-response CSV files (`consensus_heatmap_data.csv` and `dose_response_5method_regime.csv`) were invalidated because they contained PRE-D105 threshold/TY values. Current CSVs renamed with `_INVALID_preD105` suffix. A Worker is rebuilding from raw server JSON data.
   
   **Task C: OPT-1.3B s43 Registry Correction** — Completed. The analysis Worker had a bug misreading `d111_direction_ok: 0` as "all pass" instead of "zero pairs pass". Registry corrected to K≥3=0/8, K≥4=0/8.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **MMCT (Multi-Method Consensus Testing)**: 5-method framework — naive_xcorr, diff_xcorr, threshold_onset, PermG (primary), Toda-Yamamoto
   - **D105 (True 3σ)**: Changed threshold_onset significance from 2σ to 3σ. Also applies per-method BH FDR correction affecting TY at low α. Code fix in `method_ablation.py` `_onset_gen` function (2026-05-19T16:28).
   - **D111 (Direction Check)**: naive_xcorr and diff_xcorr require peak_lag > 0 (strictly greater, 0 doesn't count) for forward direction
   - **D115**: K≥4 deprecated to appendix-only after D111 audit showed near-total collapse
   - **Correct K Calculation (5-rule voting per pair)**:
     1. naive_xcorr: significant=True AND peak_lag > 0
     2. diff_xcorr: significant_fdr=True AND peak_lag > 0
     3. threshold_onset: `significant_3sigma`=True (NOT `significant`)
     4. permG: FDR p_value_fdr_bh < 0.05 AND direction="fwd"
     5. TY: significant=True
   - **4-Regime Dose-Response**: blind_zone (α≤0.45), onset (α=0.50), sync_collapse (α=0.60), detection (α≥0.75)
   - **8-pair configuration**: canary={token_entropy, ece} × downstream={distinct_1, distinct_2, distinct_3, mauve}
   - **D079**: fp32 mandatory for models ≤3B
   - **D086**: per-alpha BH FDR, n_perm=5000
   - **THREE correction layers**: D105 (3σ threshold + FDR), D111 (direction check for naive/diff), D115 (K≥4 deprecation)
   - **Data source hierarchy**: D105 3σ was only backfilled to Table 7 experiments, NOT alpha sweep. Alpha sweep registry has pre-D105 threshold/TY. D111 audit per-pair tables also have pre-D105 threshold/TY (read from server files generated before D105 fix).

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **`artifacts/d111_full_sweep_audit.md`**
     - D111 direction check audit for 9 WikiText α values (0.00, 0.05, 0.10, 0.25, 0.35, 0.50, 0.60, 0.75, 1.00)
     - Contains per-pair tables with peak_lag, significance, and direction-checked counts
     - Has CORRECT naive/diff D111 values but **PRE-D105 threshold/TY values** (read from server files)
     - Key finding: diff_fwd is 0/8 for 7/9 α values; at high α, ALL xcorr peaks at negative lags
   
   - **`artifacts/d111_a030_a040_audit.md`**
     - D111 direction check for α=0.30 and α=0.40
     - α=0.30: naive_fwd 8/8→5/8, diff_fwd 4/8→3/8, K≥3 6/8→3/8, K≥4 unchanged 3/8
     - α=0.40: naive_fwd 8/8→4/8, diff_fwd 3/8→0/8, K≥3 6/8→1/8, K≥4 1/8→0/8
     - Also PRE-D105 threshold/TY

   - **`artifacts/consensus_heatmap_data_INVALID_preD105.csv`** (renamed)
     - Same issue as above
   
   - **`artifacts/method_fpr_null.csv`**
     - FPR at α=0.00, correctly updated in prior session
   
   - **`ROADMAP.md`**
     - Updated OPT-1.3B s43 line: `| OPT-1.3B | s43 | 6/8 | **0/8** | 0/8 | ⚠️ D111: all naive/diff lag≤0, K collapsed |`
     - Table 7 has 14 experiments, many K values now in "unknown state" pending unified audit
   
   - **`registry.yaml`**
     - OPT-1.3B s43 metrics updated to: PermG_fwd=6/8, K_ge3_D111=0/8, K_ge4_D111=0/8, naive_fwd_D111=0/8, diff_fwd_D111=0/8, d111_direction_ok=0/8

4. Errors and fixes:

     - D105 reanalysis only covered Table 7, not alpha sweep → no D105-corrected alpha sweep data available

{
  "parentUuid": "d81f4ed0-22bb-48d2-841b-8939a32f247b",
  "isSidechain": false,
  "attachment": {
    "type": "file",

    "content": {
      "type": "text",
      "file": {
        "filePath": "/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0514_4/projects/distrib_canary_collapse/artifacts/d111_a030_a040_audit.md",
        "content": "# D111 Direction Check — α=0.30 and α=0.40\n\nD111 requires naive_xcorr and diff_xcorr to check peak_lag > 0 for forward direction.\n- naive_fwd: peak_lag > 0 AND significant\n- diff_fwd: peak_lag > 0 AND significant_fdr\n\n## α=0.30 (a030_s42_fp32, WikiText)\n\n| pair | naive_lag | naive_sig | naive_fwd | diff_lag | diff_sig_fdr | diff_fwd | thresh | permG | TY | K |\n|------|-----------|-----------|-----------|----------|--------------|----------|--------|-------|----|---|\n| token_entropy→distinct_1 | 1 | T | ✓ | 5 | T | ✓ | T | F | T | 4 |\n| token_entropy→distinct_2 | 1 | T | ✓ | 5 | T | ✓ | T | F | T | 4 |\n| token_entropy→distinct_3 | 1 | T | ✓ | -5 | F | ✗ | T | F | F | 2 |\n| token_entropy→mauve | 1 | T | ✓ | -3 | F | ✗ | T | F | F | 2 |\n| ece→distinct_1 | -1 | T | ✗ | -1 | T | ✗ | T | F | F | 1 |\n| ece→distinct_2 | -5 | T | ✗ | -5 | F | ✗ | T | F | T | 2 |\n| ece→distinct_3 | -5 | T | ✗ | -5 | F | ✗ | T | F | T | 2 |\n| ece→mauve | 2 | T | ✓ | 2 | T | ✓ | T | F | T | 4 |\n\n### Summary α=0.30\n\n|  | 修正前 (registry) | 修正後 (D111) | delta |\n|--|-------------------|---------------|-------|\n| naive_fwd | 8/8 | **5/8** | -3 |\n| diff_fwd | 4/8 | **3/8** | -1 |\n| threshold | 8/8 | 8/8 | 0 |\n| permG | 0/8 | 0/8 | 0 |\n| TY | 5/8 | 5/8 | 0 |\n| K≥3 | 6/8 | **3/8** | -3 |\n| K≥4 | 3/8 | **3/8** | 0 |\n\nK distribution: [4, 4, 2, 2, 1, 2, 2, 4] → K≥3=3, K≥4=3\n\nKey change: All 4 ece→* pairs have naive_lag ≤ 0 (ece leads with negative lag = reverse direction). Only ece→mauve has positive lag=2. 3 of 4 ece pairs lose naive_fwd. diff loses 1 (token_entropy→distinct_3 had lag=-5).\n\n## α=0.40 (a040_s42_fp32, WikiText)\n\n| pair | naive_lag | naive_sig | naive_fwd | diff_lag | diff_sig_fdr | diff_fwd | thresh | permG | TY | K |\n|------|-----------|-----------|-----------|----------|--------------|----------|--------|-------|----|---|\n| token_entropy→distinct_1 | 1 | T | ✓ | -1 | T | ✗ | T | F | F | 2 |\n| token_entropy→distinct_2 | 1 | T | ✓ | -5 | F | ✗ | T | F | T | 3 |\n| token_entropy→distinct_3 | 1 | T | ✓ | -5 | F | ✗ | T | F | F | 2 |\n| token_entropy→mauve | 1 | T | ✓ | -1 | T | ✗ | T | F | F | 2 |\n| ece→distinct_1 | -1 | T | ✗ | -1 | T | ✗ | T | F | T | 2 |\n| ece→distinct_2 | -5 | T | ✗ | -5 | F | ✗ | T | F | T | 2 |\n| ece→distinct_3 | -5 | T | ✗ | -5 | F | ✗ | T | F | F | 1 |\n| ece→mauve | -5 | T | ✗ | -1 | F | ✗ | T | F | T | 2 |\n\n### Summary α=0.40\n\n|  | 修正前 (registry) | 修正後 (D111) | delta |\n|--|-------------------|---------------|-------|\n| naive_fwd | 8/8 | **4/8** | -4 |\n| diff_fwd | 3/8 | **0/8** | -3 |\n| threshold | 8/8 | 8/8 | 0 |\n| permG | 0/8 | 0/8 | 0 |\n| TY | 4/8 | 4/8 | 0 |\n| K≥3 | 6/8 | **1/8** | -5 |\n| K≥4 | 1/8 | **0/8** | -1 |\n\nK distribution: [2, 3, 2, 2, 2, 2, 1, 2] → K≥3=1, K≥4=0\n\nKey change: diff_fwd drops to 0/8 — every diff peak_lag is negative. All 4 ece pairs have naive_lag ≤ 0. Combined effect collapses K≥3 from 6/8 to 1/8.\n\n## Cross-α pattern\n\nThe direction check reveals that ece→* pairs systematically have negative naive_lag (reverse direction) at both α=0.30 and α=0.40. This is consistent with the C4 audit (D111) findings. The old registry counted any significant xcorr as \"forward\" without checking lag sign, inflating naive and diff counts.\n\n| α | naive_fwd (old→new) | diff_fwd (old→new) | K≥3 (old→new) | K≥4 (old→new) |\n|---|--------------------|--------------------|----------------|----------------|\n| 0.30 | 8/8 → 5/8 | 4/8 → 3/8 | 6/8 → 3/8 | 3/8 → 3/8 |\n| 0.40 | 8/8 → 4/8 | 3/8 → 0/8 | 6/8 → 1/8 | 1/8 → 0/8 |\n",

      }

Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

314	
315	## Key Observations
316	
317	1. **D111 impact is severe**: diff_xcorr peak_lag 几乎全部 ≤ 0，diff_fwd 最高仅 2/8 (Pythia-1.4B s43)。naive_fwd 在有正 lag 的实验中保留，但多数实验 naive lag 也是负数。
318	
319	2. **D105 impact**: 3σ threshold 在 GPT-2m/OPT/SmolLM3 等实验中全部为 False，大幅削减 K 值。只有 C4 Pythia-1B (两个 seed) 和 Pythia-1.4B (两个 seed) 保持全部 8/8 threshold_3σ=True。
320	
321	3. **联合影响**: K≥3 从 mean ~5.5/8 降至 mean ~3.0/8。K≥4 从 mean ~2.8/8 降至 mean ~0.8/8。
322	
323	4. **MISSING_3SIGMA 风险**: 实验 1/2/6/8 的 threshold 使用 2σ fallback。如果实际 3σ 结果更差，K≥3 可能进一步下降：
324	   - Padding-410M s42 和 Pythia-410M s43 的 K≥3=6/8 依赖于 thresh*+permG+TY=3，如果 thresh 3σ 变 False，K≥3 将降至 4/8 (仅 permG+TY=2 的 pair 会失去 K≥3)
325	   - 但对于这两个实验，6个非 mauve pair 的 permG+TY 都=2，加上 thresh=1 恰好=3。thresh 变 False → K=2 → K≥3 降到 0/8
326	
327	5. **Pythia-1.4B s43 K 上升**: 唯一 K 值上升的实验。7/8 naive_fwd (多数 lag=1 或 lag=5) + 8/8 thresh_3σ + 6/8 permG + 4/8 TY 给出强 K 值。上升原因可能是 registry 基于不同分析文件或计算方法。
328	
329	6. **验证通过**: OPT-1.3B s43 K≥3=0/8 符合预期。
330

Pythia-410M s43 | 8/8→2/8 | 6/8 | **~0-2/8** |\n\n**已派 Worker 读取 per-pair 3σ 数据修正，预计几分钟内完成。**\n\n### 冲击分析\n\n**K≥3 全面崩溃**（修正后估计）：\n- K≥3 ≥ 5/8：仅 Pythia-1.4B s43(7/8), C4 s42(5/8), C4 s43(5/8) = **3/13**\n- K≥3 = 0-1/8：GPT-2m(0,1), OPT(0,0), SmolLM3(0), Padding(~0), Pythia-1.4B s42(1) = **7-9/13**\n- 论文原 claim \"K≥3 6-8/8 across 10/12 architectures\" → 实际 **≤3/13**\n\n**PermG 仍然稳健**（不受 D105/D111 影响）：\n- PermG ≥ 6/8: 11/13（仅 SmolLM3 3/8, Pythia-1.4B s42 2/8 例外）\n- 这是唯一可靠的跨架构指标\n\n### 根因\n1. **D111**: diff_xcorr peak_lag 几乎全为负数（max 2/8），naive_fwd 也多为负 → xcorr 方向不符合\"canary leads downstream\"的假设\n2. **D105**: 3σ threshold 在多数实验中全 False，threshold_onset 作为方法基本失效\n\n### 建议\n1. **等 MISSING_3SIGMA Worker 完成**后确认最终数值\n2. **Paper 叙事需要重大调整**：K≥3 不能再作为辅助指标——它实际上仅在 C4 和 Pythia-1.4B s43 上 work\n3. **PermG 应成为唯一主指标**，其他方法降为 \"convergent evidence at best\"\n4. diff_xcorr 和 threshold_onset 作为方法基本失效——可能需要从 MMCT\"5 方法\"降级为\"3 方法\"(PermG + TY + naive) 或更激进地重新定位",
